statements. The price transparency document confirms that practice management shares a single database with the EHR, so all billing data lives alongside clinical data.\n   141→\n   142→**Patient portal data:** Patient-submitted health information, appointment requests, prescription refill requests, secure messages.\n   143→\n   144→**Quality measures data:** eCQM data for ~20 clinical quality measures, MIPS/PQRS reporting data.\n   145→\n   146→**Audit/security data:** Audit logs, access records, authentication credentials, amendment requests.\n   147→\n   148→**Notable:** The heavy reliance on third-party services (EMR Direct for interoperability/FHIR, DrFirst for e-prescribing) means some data may flow through or be stored by these partners. The price transparency document explicitly lists these as requiring separate subscriptions, suggesting the data exchange is not purely pass-through — these third parties play active roles in data processing.\n   149→\n   150→**Information gaps:** The vendor website does not provide detailed data model documentation. It is not a single module but part of the **4MD360** product suite, which bundles the EHR with practice management, e-prescribing, patient portal, revenue cycle management, and meaningful use consulting into a unified platform sharing a single database.\n\nThe certified module (emr4MD) is part of this larger product. The full 4MD360 suite comprises six named components:\n\n- **emr4MD** — Electronic Health Records (core clinical)\n- **pm4MD** — Practice Management (scheduling, billing, claims)\n- **eRx4MD** — E-Prescribing (powered by DrFirst/Rcopia)\n- **patient4MD** — Patient Portal\n- **rcm4MD** — Revenue Cycle Management (billing services)\n- **mu4MD** — Meaningful Use Consulting\n\nSeveral key capabilities depend on third-party partners: EMR Direct provides interoperability (FHIR APIs, Direct messaging, C-CDA, EHI export) and DrFirst provides e-prescribing and drug interaction checking. These require separate subscriptions beyond the base EHR.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target users:** Physicians, clinical staff, and practice administrators in small to medium-sized ambulatory practices. The company blog meta-description states it focuses on \"solo and small internal medicine specialty\" practices. The ONC certification SED intended user description is \"Ambulatory.\"\n\n**Customer count:** Unknown and not publicly disclosed. The complete absence of third-party reviews on all major platforms (Capterra, G2, SourceForge — all zero reviews) strongly suggests a very small user base with minimal market traction.\n\n**Geography:** US-based, headquartered in Massachusetts. No information about geographic concentration of customers.\n\n**No notable customers or case studies** were found in any public source.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nThe following is based on (Prescription Benefit, Medication History, Prescription Routing)\n- Connected to 55,000+ retail pharmacies plus mail order\n- Diagnosis-driven prescription generation\n- Auto drug interaction checker (drug-to-allergy, drug-to-ADR, drug-to-drug)\n- Real-time pharmacy benefits manager (PBM) interface and formulary viewing\n- Electronic refill request processing from pharmacies\n- Patient education appointment scheduling (schedule, cancel, confirm, copy, move)\n- Automatic charge capture from emr4MD post-encounter\n- Automated claims scrubbing before submission\n- Copay collection with automatic Web-based secure two-way communication between patients and practice\n- Appointment requests and modifications\n- Prescription refill requests\n- Lab and radiology test results viewing (posted by clinical staff)\n- Clinical report access\n- Patient referral request submission\n- Automated appointment reminders\n- Refill notifications when sent to pharmacy\n- Health information access in human-readable and C-CDA format\n- Patient-submitted health information capture\n\n**Revenue Cycle Management (rcm4MD):**\n- Complete medical billing services\n- Electronic and paper claim submissions (primary, secondary, tertiary)\n- Electronic payments through ERA\n- A/R follow-up, appeals for unpaid/underpaid claims\n- Workers' Compensation and Motor Vehicle Accident claim specialists\n- Monthly patient statement generation\n- Detailed financial reports\n- Toll-free helpdesk for patient account inquiries\n\n**Interoperability & Data Exchange (via EMR Direct):**\n- FHIR APIs (referenced as both STU3 and R4 in different pages — the Open API page says STU3 Ballot, price transparency document says R4 for g(10))\n- Transitions of Care — create/transmit C-CDA referral summaries\n- Clinical Information Reconciliation from incoming C-CDAs\n- Direct messaging (h)(1)\n- Data segmentation for privacy (b)(7)/(b)(8)\n\n**Public Health Reporting:**\n- Immunization registry transmission via HL7\n- Syndromic surveillance reporting\n\n**Clinical Quality Measures:**\n- eCQM import, calculation, reporting, and filtering\n- MIPS/PQRS reporting (with separate MIPS Dashboard subscription)\n- ~20 CMS clinical quality measures tested for certification\n\n**Lab Integrations:** LabCorp, Quest Diagnostics\n\n**Clinical Terminology Support:** ICD-9, ICD-10, LOINC, SNOMED CT, NDC, RxNorm, RadLex, CPT, HCPCS, MEDCIN\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on described features and certified capabilities, emr4MD stores:\n\n**Patient demographics:** Name, address, DOB, sex, race, ethnicity, preferred language, contact information, insurance/eligibility data.\n\n**Clinical records:** Problem lists/diagnoses (ICD-9/10), medication lists (RxNorm, NDC), allergy lists (including ADRs), family </div>\n\t\t\t\t\t\t<h2>OPEN API DOCUMENTATION FOR MedNet CLIENT APPLICATION DEVELOPERS </h2>\n<h4 style=\"color:Red; font-style:italic\"> Last Updated December 13, 2023 </h4>\n<h3>About MedNet's emr4MD Product</h3>\n<p>emr4MD, version 9.10 is ONC Certified HIT solution is dynamic web-based EHR software, part of MedNet Medical Solutions' affordable EMR/EHR and Medical Billing solution, which integrates your administrative and clinical processes on one system for streamlined, consistent patient care. We customize your templates to meet your clinical needs and assist the physician with efficient documentation. We feel that EMR should not only be a tool for “meaningful” documentation, but help us in pro-actively providing disease management at the point of service. Thus we built a robust “Disease Management Engine”. </p>\n<br />\n\n\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\n<h2>Application Access Requests</h2>\n<h4 style=\"color:Red; font-style:italic\"> Last Updated December 13, 2023 </h4>\n<h3>API Syntax</h3>\n<p>The API is a read-only RESTful FHIR® STU 3 Ballot API and follows the syntax described at <a href=\"http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html\" target=\"_blank\"> http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html </a>.\n\n<br/>\nAll data access requests will be in the following format, in which the [base] URL will need to be obtained from the Data Holder, either directly or via directory information: GET [base]/[resource-specific parameters]…</p>\n<br />\n\n<h3>OPEN API DOCUMENTATION:</h3>\nPlease <a href=\"https://www.interopengine.com/2017/open-api-documentation\" 56→\t================================================== -->\n    57→\n    58→\t<!-- Header -->\n    59→\t<header id=\"header\" class=\"container\">\n    60→\t\t\n    61→\t\t<div id=\"header-inner\"  76→\t\t\n    77→\t\t<div class=\"sixteen columns\">\n    78→\t\t\t<header>\n    79→\t\t\t\t<h1 class=\"page-title page-title-with-sub\">Open API Documentation for Client Application Developers</h1>\n    80→\t\t\t\t<h4 class=\"sub-page-title\" >Interoperability Engine 2017</h4>\n    81→\t\t\t\t<h5 class=\"sub-page-title\" >Updated February 12, 2020</h5>\n    82→\t\t\t</header>\t\n    83→\t\t\t<br/>\n    84→\t\t</div>\n    85→\t\t\n    86→\n    87→\t\t\n    88→\t\t<!-- Portfolio Grid Layout -->\t\t\n    89→\t\t\t<div class=\"portfolio col-1\">\n    90→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\n    91→\t\t\t\t\t\n    92→\t\t\t\t<!-- portfolio item -->\n    93→\t\t\t\t<article class=\"portfolio-item sixteen columns\">\n    94→\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\n    95→<P>\n    96→<H4>1. Introduction</H4><P>\n    97→This guide is written for third party developers, including patients, who are developing software applications for accessing Protected Health Information (PHI) based on this documentation of an open API. \n    98→This documentation allows applications to query a public-facing API enabled by a Data Holder. \n    99→Data Holders wishing to publish such a public-facing API should have their Health IT vendor register as a developer integrator of EMR Direct Interoperability Engine services at <a href=\"https://www.emrdirect.com/subscribe-developer\">https://www.emrdirect.com/subscribe-developer</a>.<P>\n   100→ALWAYS KEEP IN MIND THAT ONLINE DATA TRANSFER IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR PERSON-TO-PERSON COMMUNICATION OF URGENT OR CRITICAL MEDICAL INFORMATION.<P>\n   101→This documentation also contains general information and important security information. General information will be marked as:\n   102→<P class=\"note\"><strong>Note:</strong></P>\n   103→<P>and important security information will be marked as:</P>\n   104→<P class=\"important\"><strong>Important:</strong></P><P>\n   105→<H4>2. General Concepts</H4><P>\n   106→    <strong>a. Application Access Requests</strong><P>\n   107→    The API is a read-only RESTful FHIR® STU 3 Ballot API and follows the syntax described at <a href=\"http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html\">http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html</a>.<P>\n   108→All data access requests will be in the following format, in which the [base] URL will need to be obtained from the Data Holder, either directly or via directory information:\n   109→<span class=\"highlight\">GET [base]/[resource-specific parameters]…</span><br /><P>\n   110→<strong>b.\tConnecting to the server</strong><P>\n   111→The server is accessed by clients through an https connection.\n   112→<P class=\"important\"> <strong>Important:</strong> Local customer security policies must be in place to prevent unauthorized monitoring or eavesdropping of connections to the server.</P>\n   113→<P class=\"note\"> <strong>Note:</strong>  Only TLS 1.2 connections are accepted.  All plaintext connections will be refused.</P>\n   114→<P class=\"note\"> <strong>Note:</strong> Please limit your connection frequency to a value appropriate for your use case. Connection attempts which are more frequent than permitted by the bandwidth allocation for the data resource are not allowed.  </P>\n   115→<P><strong>c.\tAuthentication – Obtaining an Access Token</strong><P>\n   116→Client authentication can be performed using a username and strong password. A healthcare organization may reuse existing patient portal credentials for this purpose, in which case the authenticated username maps to a unique patient portal user on the resource holder’s side. The end user should obtain these credentials directly from the healthcare organizations from which they wish to access data.<P>\n   117→Prior to making API requests, the client application must obtain an Access Token from the associated Authorization Server. The client software must support the OAuth 2.0 authorization code grant flow as detailed in RFC 6749. If the client application does not have a client ID and client secret for this purpose, the client application may obtain a client ID and client secret using the dynamic client registration protocol by submitting the required client information to the registration endpoint as detailed in RFC 7591.<P>\n   118→Each healthcare organization will have a unique base URL to access its Authorization Server. The required endpoint URLs are as follows:<P>\n   119→<TABLE>\n   120→<TR>\n   121→    <TH>Endpoint</TH><TH>URL</TH></TR>\n   122→<TR><TD>Authorization</TD>\t<TD>https://[baseOAuthURL]/authz</TD></TR>\n   123→<TR><TD>Token</TD>\t<TD>https://[baseOAuthURL]/token</TD></TR>\n   124→<TR><TD>Registration</TD>\t<TD>https://[baseOAuthURL]/register</TD></TR>\n   125→</TABLE>\n   126→<P>\n   127→When the end user is directed to the authorization endpoint, the user will be presented with a login screen where they can enter their credentials for the healthcare organization they are accessing. If the correct credentials are supplied and the end user grants access to the client application, an authorization code will be returned to the client that the client application can use to obtain an access token through the token endpoint.<P>\n   128→All requests to the API must include the access token transmitted in the Authorization header of the HTTP request as a bearer token as illustrated in RFC 6749. If the access token is missing, expired, or otherwise not valid for the requested operation, the API will return a 401 Unauthorized response.<P>\n   129→<H4>3. API Details</H4><P>\n   130→<strong>a. Query a Specific Data Category Resource</strong><P>\n   131→Client software must be capable of making HTTPS RESTful requests in accordance with the FHIR specification and consuming the following FHIR Resources in order to support the Common Clinical Data Set (CCDS):<P>\n   132→<TABLE>\n   133→<TR>\n   134→<TH>Common Clinical Data Set Element\t</TH><TH>Resource\t</TH><TH>Resource data element (if applicable)</TH></TR>\n   135→<TR>\n   136→<TD>Patient Name\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.name</TD></TR>\n   137→<TR>\n   138→<TD>Sex\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.gender</TD></TR>\n   139→<TR>\n   140→<TD>Date of Birth\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.birthDate</TD></TR>\n   141→<TR>\n   142→<TD>Race\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.extension</TD></TR>\n   143→<TR>\n   144→<TD>Ethnicity\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.extension</TD></TR>\n   145→<TR>\n   146→<TD>Preferred Language\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.communication</TD></TR>\n   147→<TR>\n   148→    <TD>Smoking Status\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n   149→<TR>\n   150→<TD>Problems\t</TD><TD>Condition\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n   151→<TR>\n   152→<TD>Medications\t</TD><TD>MedicationStatement</TD><TD></TD></TR>\t\n   153→<TR>\n   154→<TD>Medication Allergies\t</TD><TD>AllergyIntolerance\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n   155→<TR>\n   156→<TD>Laboratory Tests\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD>Observation.code</TD></TR>\n   157→<TR>\n   158→<TD>Laboratory Values Results\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD>Observation.value[x]</TD></TR>\n   159→<TR>\n   160→<TD>Vital Signs\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n   161→<TR>\n   162→<TD>Procedures\t</TD><TD>Procedure\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n   163→<TR>\n   164→<TD>Care Team Members\t</TD><TD>CareTeam</TD><TD></TD></TR>\t\n   165→<TR>\n   166→<TD>Immunizations\t</TD><TD>Immunization</TD><TD></TD></TR>\t\n   167→<TR>\n   168→<TD>Unique Device Identifiers\t</TD><TD>Device\t</TD><TD>Device.udiCarrier</TD></TR>\n   169→<TR>\n   170→<TD>Assessment and Plan\t</TD><TD>CarePlan</TD><TD></TD></TR>\t\n   171→<TR>\n   172→<TD>Goals\t</TD><TD>Goal\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n   173→<TR>\n   174→<TD>Health Concerns</TD><TD>\tCondition</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n   175→</TABLE><P>\n   176→General specifications for FHIR resources and the associated data elements can be found at <a href=\"http://www.hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/resourcelist.html\">http://www.hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/resourcelist.html</a>.<P>\n   177→Additional information for the FHIR Resource or Data Element used to represent each data category can be found at the URLs listed in the following table:<P>\n   178→<TABLE>\n   179→<TR>\n   180→<TH>Common Clinical Data Set Element\t</TH><TH>Resource or Data Element\t</TH><TH>URL for Additional Information</TH></TR>\n   181→<TR>\n   182→<TD>Patient Name\t</TD><TD>Patient.name\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/patient-definitions.html#Patient.name</TD></TR>\n   183→\n   184→<TR>\n   185→<TD>Sex\t</TD><TD>Patient.gender\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/patient-definitions.html#Patient.gender</TD></TR>\n   186→\n   187→<TR>\n   188→<TD>Date of Birth\t</TD><TD>Patient.birthDate\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/patient-definitions.html#Patient.birthDate</TD></TR>\n   189→\n   190→<TR>\n   191→<TD>Race\t</TD><TD>Patient.extension\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/extension-us-core-race.html</TD></TR>\n   192→\n   193→<TR>\n   194→<TD>Ethnicity\t</TD><TD>Patient.extension\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/extension-us-core-ethnicity.html</TD></TR>\n   195→\n   196→<TR>\n   197→<TD>Preferred Language\t</TD><TD>Patient.communication\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/patient-definitions.html#Patient.communication</TD></TR>\n   198→\n   199→<TR>\n   200→<TD>Smoking Status\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/observation.html</TD></TR>\n   201→\n   202→<TR>\n   203→<TD>Problems\t</TD><TD>Condition\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/condition.html</TD></TR>\n   204→\n   205→<TR>\n   206→<TD>Medications\t</TD><TD>MedicationStatement\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/medicationstatement.html</TD></TR>\n   207→\n   208→<TR>\n   209→<TD>Medication Allergies\t</TD><TD>AllergyIntolerance\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/allergyintolerance.html</TD></TR>\n   210→\n   211→<TR>\n   212→<TD>Laboratory Tests\t</TD><TD>Observation.code\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/observation-definitions.html#Observation.code</TD></TR>\n   213→\n   214→<TR>\n   215→<TD>Laboratory Values Results\t</TD><TD>Observation.value[x]\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/observation-definitions.html#Observation.value_x_</TD></TR>\n   216→\n   217→<TR>\n   218→<TD>Vital Signs\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/observation.html</TD></TR>\n   219→\n   220→<TR>\n   221→<TD>Procedures\t</TD><TD>Procedure\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/procedure.html</TD></TR>\n   222→\n   223→<TR>\n   224→<TD>Care Team Members\t</TD><TD>CareTeam\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/careteam.html</TD></TR>\n   225→\n   226→<TR>\n   227→<TD>Immunizations\t</TD><TD>Immunization\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/immunization.html</TD></TR>\n   228→\n   229→<TR>\n   230→<TD>Unique Device Identifiers\t</TD><TD>Device.udiCarrier\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/device-definitions.html#Device.udiCarrier</TD></TR>\n   231→\n   232→<TR>\n   233→<TD>Assessment and Plan\t</TD><TD>CarePlan\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/careplan.html</TD></TR>\n   234→\n   235→<TR>\n   236→<TD>Goals\t</TD><TD>Goal\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/goal.html</TD></TR>\n   237→\n   238→<TR>\n   239→<TD>Health Concerns\t</TD><TD>Condition\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/condition.html</TD></TR>\n   240→\n   241→</TABLE>\n   242→<P class=\"note\">\n   243→<strong>Note:</strong>  Authentication certificates or passwords can be reset by the Data Holder.</P>\n   244→<P>\n   245→<strong>b.\tPatient Selection</strong>\n   246→<P>\n   247→To search for patients, the application should request a bundle of Patient resources matching suitable search criteria. To facilitate this, the following optional search parameters can be applied to the Patient resource:\n   248→<P>\n   249→<TABLE>\n   250→<TR>\n   251→    <TH>Resource Data Element\t</TH><TH>Search Parameter</TH></TR>\n   252→<TR>\n   253→<TD>Patient.name.family\t</TD><TD>family</TD></TR>\n   254→<TR>\n   255→<TD>Patient.birthDate\t</TD><TD>birthdate</TD></TR></TABLE> \n   256→<P>\n   257→For example, to retrieve a bundle of Patient resources to which the user is authorized, where the patient’s last name is Smith and the patient was born on July 4, 1976, the request could be formatted as:<P>\n   258→<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\n   259→https://[baseURL]/Patient?family=Smith&amp;birthdate=1976-07-04</span>\n   260→<br />\n   261→<P>\n   262→The API will return a bundle of all patients (possibly zero) matching the search criteria. Only patients for which the user has been authorized access will be included in the results. Each patient returned in the search results is assigned a unique patient ID that can be found in the Patient.id element of the corresponding Patient resource. This patient ID can be included in subsequent requests to retrieve additional resources for that specific patient. \n   263→<P>\n   264→<strong>c.\tQuery for a Specific Data Category</strong>\n   265→<P>\n   266→The Patient resource can be retrieved by specifying a specific patient ID or by performing a search as discussed in section 5(b). The remaining resource types listed in Section 5(a) can be accessed for a specific patient as a Bundle of resources by performing a search by resource type or by patient compartment and specifying the patient ID in the request. For example, to retrieve a bundle of Immunization resources containing all available immunization history for Patient 1234, the request could be formatted as:<P>\n   267→<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\n   268→https://[baseURL]/Immunization?patient=1234</span>\n   269→<p style=\"margin-left: 80px\">\n   270→or (equivalently) <P>\n   271→<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\n   272→https://[baseURL]/Patient/1234/Immunization</span>\n   273→<br />\n   274→<P>\n   275→The following search terms can be used to isolate results for a single CCDS category in cases where two or more CCDS categories are represented by a single Resource type:\n   276→<P>\n   277→<TABLE>\n   278→<TR>\n   279→<TH>CCDS Element\t</TH><TH>Search Term</TH></TR>\n   280→<TR>\n   281→<TD>Health Concern\t</TD><TD>category=http://argonaut.hl7.org/extension-codes|health-concern</TD></TR>\n   282→<TR>\n   283→<TD>Problem\t</TD><TD>category=http://argonaut.hl7.org/extension-codes|problem</TD></TR>\n   284→<TR>\n   285→<TD>Smoking Status\t</TD><TD>code=http://loinc.org|72166-2</TD></TR>\n   286→<TR>\n   287→<TD>Vital Signs\t</TD><TD>category=http://hl7.org/fhir/observation-category|vital-signs</TD></TR>\n   288→<TR>\n   289→<TD>Laboratory Tests &amp; Laboratory Values/Results\t</TD><TD>category=http://hl7.org/fhir/observation-category|laboratory</TD></TR></TABLE>\n   290→<P>\n   291→For example, to retrieve the Smoking Status for Patient 1234, the request could be formatted as:<P>\n   292→<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\n   293→https://[baseURL]/Patient/1234/Observation?code=http://loinc.org|72166-2</span>\n   294→<br />\n   295→<P>\n   296→Each search request will return a bundle of zero or more results meeting the search criteria.\n   297→<P>\n   298→<strong>d.\tQuery for All Data as a CCDA document</strong>\n   299→<P>\n   300→CCDA documents can be accessed within DocumentReference resources. CCDAs are categorized as “Summary of Episode” Notes with LOINC code 34133-9. For example, to request a CCDA document covering all dates for patient 1234, the query could be formatted as:<P>\n   301→<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\n   302→https://[baseURL]/DocumentReference?patient=1234&amp;type=http://loinc.org|34133-9</span>\n   303→<br />\n   304→<P>\n   305→The Base64 encoded CCDA XML data can be found in the DocumentReference.content.attchment.data element of the returned DocumentReference resource\n   306→<P>\n   307→<strong>e.\tQuery for a Specific Date or Date Range</strong>\n   308→<P>\n   309→    The CCDS data categories or CCDA documents returned by the API may be limited by date by specifying either (1) a specific date or (2) start and/or end dates. These dates can be included as FHIR STU3 search parameters as defined at <a href=\"http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html#search\">http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html#search</a>. Specifying a date or date range in the request is optional. The following table lists the supported search parameters for this purpose:\n   310→<P>\n   311→<TABLE>\n   312→<TR>\n   313→<TH>Resource Data Element\t</TH><TH>Search Parameter</TH></TR>\n   314→<TR>\n   315→<TD>AllergyIntolerance.reaction.onset\t</TD><TD>onset</TD></TR>\n   316→<TR>\n   317→<TD>CarePlan.period\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n   318→<TR>\n   319→<TD>CareTeam.period\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n   320→<TR>\n   321→<TD>Condition.onset\t</TD><TD>onset-date</TD></TR>\n   322→<TR>\n   323→<TD>Goal.target\t</TD><TD>targetdate</TD></TR>\n   324→<TR>\n   325→<TD>Immunization.date\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n   326→<TR>\n   327→<TD>MedicationStatement.effective\t</TD><TD>effective</TD></TR>\n   328→<TR>\n   329→<TD>Observation.effective\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n   330→<TR>\n   331→<TD>Procedure.performed\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n   332→<TR>\n   333→<TD>DocuementReference.period\t</TD><TD>period</TD></TR></TABLE>\n   334→<P>\t\n   335→<strong>f.\tError Handling</strong>\n   336→<P>\n   337→If the access token used in the request is invalid, expired, or the user has not been authorized to access the requested Resource, the API will return a 401 Unauthorized HTTP response.\n   338→<P>\n   339→If the request cannot be processed for other reasons (temporarily unavailable, unsupported resource type, system error, etc.), the API will return a 400 Bad Request HTTP response containing an OperationOutcome Resource with additional information regarding the issue contained in the OperationOutcome.issue element.\n   340→<P>\n   341→Handling of OAuth-related errors is detailed in RFC 6749.\n   342→<P>\n   343→Handling of dynamic client registration errors is detailed in RFC 7591.\n   344→<P>\n   345→<H4>4.\tFrequently Asked Questions</H4>\n   346→<P>\n   347→<strong>a.\tHow do I access production API resources with my client application?</strong>\n   348→<P>\n   349→Please obtain [base] URL resource information from a specific healthcare provider organization when you are ready to begin allowing client end users to access PHI with production credentials. Each clinical data source accessible through the API will have a unique Base URL.\n   350→<P>\n   351→<strong>b.\tHow extensible is the Interoperability Engine API?</strong>\n   352→<P>\n   353→API resources are modeled after the HL7 FHIR Standard for Trial Use 3 (STU 3) Ballot which is in ballot stage as of this writing. This internationally-recognized API standard has been extended to include the additional data categories needed for use in systems certifying to the 2015 Edition Certification Criteria’s Common Clinical Data Set requirements. The API is designed to optimally interface with client applications while being comprehensive as a 2015 Edition solution for implementers wishing to rely on EMR Direct Interoperability Engine for Application Access services. This guide will help you access the data categories enabled by 2015 Edition Application Access APIs.\n   354→<P>\n   355→<strong>c.\tHow do end users and applications authenticate to the API?</strong>\n   356→<P>\n   357→The API is designed to support existing patient portal credentials via the OAuth 2.0 authorization framework as per RFC 6749. The OAuth server also supports dynamic client registration as per RFC 7591. \n   358→<P>\n   359→<strong>d.\tWhat data is available through the API?</strong>\n   360→<P>\n   361→The API will return all properly formatted data provided by a connected data source system in response to a submitted query. Healthcare organizations may have their own policies and/or safety best practices that will dictate what data can be sent and when data is considered complete and/or ready to be sent. Please contact a healthcare organization directly for questions related to their specific policies.\n   362→<P>\n   363→    <strong>e. What additional options are offered?</strong>\n   364→<P>\n   365→    Interoperability Engine 2017 also supports FHIR DSTU2 (with the Argonaut Data Query IG profiles),\n   366→    the SMART App Launch Framework (both standalone launch and EHR launch),\n   367→    SMART clinical scopes (patient, user, and system scopes), Open ID Connect, UMA (with the HEART profile),\n   368→    OAuth 2.0 client credentials flow, OAuth 2.0 refresh tokens, OAuth 2.0 revocation, \n   369→    the Unified Data Access Profiles, manual client registration, sandbox access for testing, and more. Please contact the Data Holder\n   370→    to determine which additional options have been enabled by the Data Holder for a particular endpoint.\n   371→</P>\n   372→    Terms of Use: <A HREF=\"http://www.interopengine.com/open-api-terms\">http://www.interopengine.com/open-api-terms</A>\n   373→<P>\n   374→\n   375→Trademark Notice: Interoperability Engine and the Interoperability Engine gears logo are trademarks of\n   376→EMR Direct. role=\"main\">\n\t\t\n\t\t<div class=\"sixteen columns\">\n\t\t\t<header>\n\t\t\t\t<h1 class=\"page-title page-title-with-sub\">Open API Documentation for Client Application Developers</h1>\n\t\t\t\t<h4 class=\"sub-page-title\" >Interoperability Engine 2017</h4>\n\t\t\t\t<h5 class=\"sub-page-title\" >Updated February 12, 2020</h5>\n\t\t\t</header>\t\n\t\t\t<br/>\n\t\t</div>\n\t\t\n\n\t\t\n\t\t<!-- Portfolio Grid Layout -->\t\t\n\t\t\t<div class=\"portfolio col-1\">\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\n\t\t\t\t\t\n\t\t\t\t<!-- portfolio item -->\n\t\t\t\t<article class=\"portfolio-item sixteen columns\">\n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\n<P>\n<H4>1. Introduction</H4><P>\nThis guide is written for third party developers, including patients, who are developing software applications for accessing Protected Health Information (PHI) based on this documentation of an open API. \nThis documentation allows applications to query a public-facing API enabled by a Data Holder. \nData Holders wishing to publish such a public-facing API should have their Health IT vendor register as a developer integrator of EMR Direct Interoperability Engine services at <a href=\"https://www.emrdirect.com/subscribe-developer\">https://www.emrdirect.com/subscribe-developer</a>.<P>\nALWAYS KEEP IN MIND THAT ONLINE DATA TRANSFER IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR PERSON-TO-PERSON COMMUNICATION OF URGENT OR CRITICAL MEDICAL INFORMATION.<P>\nThis documentation also contains general information and important security information. General information will be marked as:\n<P class=\"note\"><strong>Note:</strong></P>\n<P>and important security information will be marked as:</P>\n<P class=\"important\"><strong>Important:</strong></P><P>\n<H4>2. General Concepts</H4><P>\n    <strong>a. Application Access Requests</strong><P>\n    The API is a read-only RESTful FHIR® STU 3 Ballot API and follows the syntax described at <a href=\"http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html\">http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html</a>.<P>\nAll data access requests will be in the following format, in which the [base] URL will need to be obtained from the Data Holder, either directly or via directory information:\n<span class=\"highlight\">GET [base]/[resource-specific parameters]…</span><br /><P>\n<strong>b.\tConnecting to the server</strong><P>\nThe server is accessed by clients through an https connection.\n<P class=\"important\"> <strong>Important:</strong> Local customer security policies must be in place to prevent unauthorized monitoring or eavesdropping of connections to the server.</P>\n<P class=\"note\"> <strong>Note:</strong>  Only TLS 1.2 connections are accepted.  All plaintext connections will be refused.</P>\n<P class=\"note\"> <strong>Note:</strong> Please limit your connection frequency to a value appropriate for your use case. Connection attempts which are more frequent than permitted by the bandwidth allocation for the data resource are not allowed.  </P>\n<P><strong>c.\tAuthentication – Obtaining an Access Token</strong><P>\nClient authentication can be performed using a username and strong password. A healthcare organization may reuse existing patient portal credentials for this purpose, in which case the authenticated username maps to a unique patient portal user on the resource holder’s side. The end user should obtain these credentials directly from the healthcare organizations from which they wish to access data.<P>\nPrior to making API requests, the client application must obtain an Access Token from the associated Authorization Server. The client software must support the OAuth 2.0 authorization code grant flow as detailed in RFC 6749. If the client application does not have a client ID and client secret for this purpose, the client application may obtain a client ID and client secret using the dynamic client registration protocol by submitting the required client information to the registration endpoint as detailed in RFC 7591.<P>\nEach healthcare organization will have a unique base URL to access its Authorization Server. The required endpoint URLs are as follows:<P>\n<TABLE>\n<TR>\n    <TH>Endpoint</TH><TH>URL</TH></TR>\n<TR><TD>Authorization</TD>\t<TD>https://[baseOAuthURL]/authz</TD></TR>\n<TR><TD>Token</TD>\t<TD>https://[baseOAuthURL]/token</TD></TR>\n<TR><TD>Registration</TD>\t<TD>https://[baseOAuthURL]/register</TD></TR>\n</TABLE>\n<P>\nWhen the end user is directed to the authorization endpoint, the user will be presented with a login screen where they can enter their credentials for the healthcare organization they are accessing. If the correct credentials are supplied and the end user grants access to the client application, an authorization code will be returned to the client that the client application can use to obtain an access token through the token endpoint.<P>\nAll requests to the API must include the access token transmitted in the Authorization header of the HTTP request as a bearer token as illustrated in RFC 6749. If the access token is missing, expired, or otherwise not valid for the requested operation, the API will return a 401 Unauthorized response.<P>\n<H4>3. API Details</H4><P>\n<strong>a. Query a Specific Data Category Resource</strong><P>\nClient software must be capable of making HTTPS RESTful requests in accordance with the FHIR specification and consuming the following FHIR Resources in order to support the Common Clinical Data Set (CCDS):<P>\n<TABLE>\n<TR>\n<TH>Common Clinical Data Set Element\t</TH><TH>Resource\t</TH><TH>Resource data element (if applicable)</TH></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Patient Name\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.name</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Sex\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.gender</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Date of Birth\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.birthDate</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Race\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.extension</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Ethnicity\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.extension</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Preferred Language\t</TD><TD>Patient\t</TD><TD>Patient.communication</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n    <TD>Smoking Status\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Problems\t</TD><TD>Condition\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Medications\t</TD><TD>MedicationStatement</TD><TD></TD></TR>\t\n<TR>\n<TD>Medication Allergies\t</TD><TD>AllergyIntolerance\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Laboratory Tests\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD>Observation.code</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Laboratory Values Results\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD>Observation.value[x]</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Vital Signs\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Procedures\t</TD><TD>Procedure\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Care Team Members\t</TD><TD>CareTeam</TD><TD></TD></TR>\t\n<TR>\n<TD>Immunizations\t</TD><TD>Immunization</TD><TD></TD></TR>\t\n<TR>\n<TD>Unique Device Identifiers\t</TD><TD>Device\t</TD><TD>Device.udiCarrier</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Assessment and Plan\t</TD><TD>CarePlan</TD><TD></TD></TR>\t\n<TR>\n<TD>Goals\t</TD><TD>Goal\t</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Health Concerns</TD><TD>\tCondition</TD><TD></TD></TR>\n</TABLE><P>\nGeneral specifications for FHIR resources and the associated data elements can be found at <a href=\"http://www.hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/resourcelist.html\">http://www.hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/resourcelist.html</a>.<P>\nAdditional information for the FHIR Resource or Data Element used to represent each data category can be found at the URLs listed in the following table:<P>\n<TABLE>\n<TR>\n<TH>Common Clinical Data Set Element\t</TH><TH>Resource or Data Element\t</TH><TH>URL for Additional Information</TH></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Patient Name\t</TD><TD>Patient.name\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/patient-definitions.html#Patient.name</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Sex\t</TD><TD>Patient.gender\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/patient-definitions.html#Patient.gender</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Date of Birth\t</TD><TD>Patient.birthDate\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/patient-definitions.html#Patient.birthDate</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Race\t</TD><TD>Patient.extension\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/extension-us-core-race.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Ethnicity\t</TD><TD>Patient.extension\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/extension-us-core-ethnicity.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Preferred Language\t</TD><TD>Patient.communication\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/patient-definitions.html#Patient.communication</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Smoking Status\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/observation.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Problems\t</TD><TD>Condition\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/condition.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Medications\t</TD><TD>MedicationStatement\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/medicationstatement.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Medication Allergies\t</TD><TD>AllergyIntolerance\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/allergyintolerance.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Laboratory Tests\t</TD><TD>Observation.code\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/observation-definitions.html#Observation.code</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Laboratory Values Results\t</TD><TD>Observation.value[x]\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/observation-definitions.html#Observation.value_x_</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Vital Signs\t</TD><TD>Observation\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/observation.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Procedures\t</TD><TD>Procedure\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/procedure.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Care Team Members\t</TD><TD>CareTeam\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/careteam.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Immunizations\t</TD><TD>Immunization\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/immunization.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Unique Device Identifiers\t</TD><TD>Device.udiCarrier\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/device-definitions.html#Device.udiCarrier</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Assessment and Plan\t</TD><TD>CarePlan\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/careplan.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Goals\t</TD><TD>Goal\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/goal.html</TD></TR>\n\n<TR>\n<TD>Health Concerns\t</TD><TD>Condition\t</TD><TD>http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/condition.html</TD></TR>\n\n</TABLE>\n<P class=\"note\">\n<strong>Note:</strong>  Authentication certificates or passwords can be reset by the Data Holder.</P>\n<P>\n<strong>b.\tPatient Selection</strong>\n<P>\nTo search for patients, the application should request a bundle of Patient resources matching suitable search criteria. To facilitate this, the following optional search parameters can be applied to the Patient resource:\n<P>\n<TABLE>\n<TR>\n    <TH>Resource Data Element\t</TH><TH>Search Parameter</TH></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Patient.name.family\t</TD><TD>family</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Patient.birthDate\t</TD><TD>birthdate</TD></TR></TABLE> \n<P>\nFor example, to retrieve a bundle of Patient resources to which the user is authorized, where the patient’s last name is Smith and the patient was born on July 4, 1976, the request could be formatted as:<P>\n<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\nhttps://[baseURL]/Patient?family=Smith&amp;birthdate=1976-07-04</span>\n<br />\n<P>\nThe API will return a bundle of all patients (possibly zero) matching the search criteria. Only patients for which the user has been authorized access will be included in the results. Each patient returned in the search results is assigned a unique patient ID that can be found in the Patient.id element of the corresponding Patient resource. This patient ID can be included in subsequent requests to retrieve additional resources for that specific patient. \n<P>\n<strong>c.\tQuery for a Specific Data Category</strong>\n<P>\nThe Patient resource can be retrieved by specifying a specific patient ID or by performing a search as discussed in section 5(b). The remaining resource types listed in Section 5(a) can be accessed for a specific patient as a Bundle of resources by performing a search by resource type or by patient compartment and specifying the patient ID in the request. For example, to retrieve a bundle of Immunization resources containing all available immunization history for Patient 1234, the request could be formatted as:<P>\n<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\nhttps://[baseURL]/Immunization?patient=1234</span>\n<p style=\"margin-left: 80px\">\nor (equivalently) <P>\n<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\nhttps://[baseURL]/Patient/1234/Immunization</span>\n<br />\n<P>\nThe following search terms can be used to isolate results for a single CCDS category in cases where two or more CCDS categories are represented by a single Resource type:\n<P>\n<TABLE>\n<TR>\n<TH>CCDS Element\t</TH><TH>Search Term</TH></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Health Concern\t</TD><TD>category=http://argonaut.hl7.org/extension-codes|health-concern</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Problem\t</TD><TD>category=http://argonaut.hl7.org/extension-codes|problem</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Smoking Status\t</TD><TD>code=http://loinc.org|72166-2</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Vital Signs\t</TD><TD>category=http://hl7.org/fhir/observation-category|vital-signs</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Laboratory Tests &amp; Laboratory Values/Results\t</TD><TD>category=http://hl7.org/fhir/observation-category|laboratory</TD></TR></TABLE>\n<P>\nFor example, to retrieve the Smoking Status for Patient 1234, the request could be formatted as:<P>\n<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\nhttps://[baseURL]/Patient/1234/Observation?code=http://loinc.org|72166-2</span>\n<br />\n<P>\nEach search request will return a bundle of zero or more results meeting the search criteria.\n<P>\n<strong>d.\tQuery for All Data as a CCDA document</strong>\n<P>\nCCDA documents can be accessed within DocumentReference resources. CCDAs are categorized as “Summary of Episode” Notes with LOINC code 34133-9. For example, to request a CCDA document covering all dates for patient 1234, the query could be formatted as:<P>\n<span class=\"highlight\" style=\"margin-left: 40px\">\nhttps://[baseURL]/DocumentReference?patient=1234&amp;type=http://loinc.org|34133-9</span>\n<br />\n<P>\nThe Base64 encoded CCDA XML data can be found in the DocumentReference.content.attchment.data element of the returned DocumentReference resource\n<P>\n<strong>e.\tQuery for a Specific Date or Date Range</strong>\n<P>\n    The CCDS data categories or CCDA documents returned by the API may be limited by date by specifying either (1) a specific date or (2) start and/or end dates. These dates can be included as FHIR STU3 search parameters as defined at <a href=\"http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html#search\">http://hl7.org/fhir/2016Sep/http.html#search</a>. Specifying a date or date range in the request is optional. The following table lists the supported search parameters for this purpose:\n<P>\n<TABLE>\n<TR>\n<TH>Resource Data Element\t</TH><TH>Search Parameter</TH></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>AllergyIntolerance.reaction.onset\t</TD><TD>onset</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>CarePlan.period\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>CareTeam.period\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Condition.onset\t</TD><TD>onset-date</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Goal.target\t</TD><TD>targetdate</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Immunization.date\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>MedicationStatement.effective\t</TD><TD>effective</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Observation.effective\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>Procedure.performed\t</TD><TD>date</TD></TR>\n<TR>\n<TD>DocuementReference.period\t</TD><TD>period</TD></TR></TABLE>\n<P>\t\n<strong>f.\tError Handling</strong>\n<P>\nIf the access token used in the request is invalid, expired, or the user has not been authorized to access the requested Resource, the API will return a 401 Unauthorized HTTP response.\n<P>\nIf the request cannot be processed for other reasons (temporarily unavailable, unsupported resource type, system error, etc.), the API will return a 400 Bad Request HTTP response containing an OperationOutcome Resource with additional information regarding the issue contained in the OperationOutcome.issue element.\n<P>\nHandling of OAuth-related errors is detailed in RFC 6749.\n<P>\nHandling of dynamic client registration errors is detailed in RFC 7591.\n<P>\n<H4>4.\tFrequently Asked Questions</H4>\n<P>\n<strong>a.\tHow do I access production API resources with my client application?</strong>\n<P>\nPlease obtain [base] URL resource information from a specific healthcare provider organization when you are ready to begin allowing client end users to access PHI with production credentials. Each clinical data source accessible through the API will have a unique Base URL.\n<P>\n<strong>b.\tHow extensible is the Interoperability Engine API?</strong>\n<P>\nAPI resources are modeled after the HL7 FHIR Standard for Trial Use 3 (STU 3) Ballot which is in ballot stage as of this writing. This internationally-recognized API standard has been extended to include the additional data categories needed for use in systems certifying to the 2015 Edition Certification Criteria’s Common Clinical Data Set requirements. The API is designed to optimally interface with client applications while being comprehensive as a 2015 Edition solution for implementers wishing to rely on EMR Direct Interoperability Engine for Application Access services. This guide will help you access the data categories enabled by 2015 Edition Application Access APIs.\n<P>\n<strong>c.\tHow do end users and applications authenticate to the API?</strong>\n<P>\nThe API is designed to support existing patient portal credentials via the OAuth 2.0 authorization framework as per RFC 6749. The OAuth server also supports dynamic client registration as per RFC 7591. \n<P>\n<strong>d.\tWhat data is available through the API?</strong>\n<P>\nThe API will return all properly formatted data provided by a connected data source system in response to a submitted query. Healthcare organizations may have their own policies and/or safety best practices that will dictate what data can be sent and when data is considered complete and/or ready to be sent. Please contact a healthcare organization directly for questions related to their specific policies.\n<P>\n    <strong>e. What additional options are offered?</strong>\n<P>\n    Interoperability Engine 2017 also supports FHIR DSTU2 (with the Argonaut Data Query IG profiles),\n    the SMART App Launch Framework (both standalone launch and EHR launch),\n    SMART clinical scopes (patient, user, and system scopes), Open ID Connect, UMA (with the HEART profile),\n    OAuth 2.0 client credentials flow, OAuth 2.0 refresh tokens, OAuth 2.0 revocation, \n    the Unified Data Access Profiles, manual client registration, sandbox access for testing, and more. Please contact the Data Holder\n    to determine which additional options have been enabled by the Data Holder for a particular endpoint.\n</P>\n    Terms of Use: <A HREF=\"http://www.interopengine.com/open-api-terms\">http://www.interopengine.com/open-api-terms</A>\n<P>\n\nTrademark Notice: Interoperability Engine and the Interoperability Engine gears logo are trademarks of\nEMR Direct. HL7 and FHIR are registered trademarks of Health Level Seven International.\n\n<P>Copyright Notice: &copy; 2026 EMR Direct. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01PGUnAiY2dUmsWfZwdez66p","type":"tool_result","content":"\t<!-- Basic Page Needs\n \t================================================== -->\n\t \n\t Open API Documentation | EMR Direct \n\t \n\t\n\t \n\t\t \n\t \n\t<!-- Mobile Specific Metas\n \t================================================== -->\n\t \n\t<!-- CSS\n \t================================================== -->\n\t \n\t \n\t \n\t\n\t<!-- Favicons\n\t================================================== -->\n\t \n\t\n\t<!-- Fonts\n\t================================================== -->\t\n\t \n\t \n\t\t\n\t\n\t \n\t \n\t\n\t \n\t \n\t \n\t\n \n \n \n\t<!-- Primary Page Layout\n\t================================================== -->\n\t \n\t \n\t\t\n \n \t\t\t \r\n\t\t\t \r\n\t\t\t \r\n \n\t\t\n\t \n\t \n\t \n\t \n\t\t\n \n \n Open API Documentation for Client Application Developers \n Interoperability Engine 2026 \n Updated February 5, 2026 \n \t\n \n \n \t\t\n \n \n \n\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\t\n 1. Introduction \nThis guide is written for third party developers, including patients, who are developing software applications for accessing Protected Health Information (PHI) based on this public API documentation which allows applications to query health data secured by a Data Holder. Data Holders wishing to publish such a public-facing API should have their Health IT vendor register as a developer integrator of EMR Direct Interoperability Engine services at https://www.emrdirect.com/subscribe-developer . \nALWAYS KEEP IN MIND THAT ONLINE DATA TRANSFER IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR PERSON-TO-PERSON COMMUNICATION OF URGENT OR CRITICAL MEDICAL INFORMATION. \nThis documentation contains general information and important security information. General information will be marked as:\n Note: \n and important security information will be marked as: \n Important: \n \n 2. General Concepts \n a. Application Access Requests \n The API is a read-only RESTful HL7 FHIR® R4 API and follows the syntax described at https://hl7.org/fhir/R4/http.html . \nAll RESTful data access requests will be in the following format, in which the [base] URL will need to be obtained from the Data Holder, either directly or via directory information:\n GET [base]/[resource-specific parameters]… \n FHIR Bulk Data Access Group export is also supported as described in the Bulk Data Access IG. \n b.\tConnecting to the server \nThe server is accessed by clients through an https connection.\n Important: Local customer security policies must be in place to prevent unauthorized monitoring or eavesdropping of connections to the server. \n Important: Only TLS 1.2 connections are accepted. All plaintext connections will be refused. \n Note: Please limit your connection frequency to a value appropriate for your use case. Connection attempts which are more frequent than permitted by the bandwidth allocation for the data resource are not allowed. \n c. Authentication – Obtaining an Access Token \nPrior to making API requests, the client application must obtain an Access Token from the associated Authorization Server. The client software must support either the OAuth 2.0 authorization code grant or client credentials grant flow as detailed in RFC 6749 . For authorization code flow, a client ID and client secret or a client ID and private key JWT can be used for authentication. If the client application does not have a client ID, the client application may obtain a client ID and (when required), client secret, using the dynamic client registration protocol by submitting the required client information to the registration endpoint as detailed in RFC 7591 , by accessing the registration endpoint URL with a browser and completing the online form, by completing the manual registration process at the EMR Direct website, or by using UDAP Dynamic Client Registration . Please see section 3(j) below for the list Client app attributes required for registration. For client credentials flow, a client ID and registered public key are required. Public keys for client credentials grant access may be registered using UDAP Dynamic Client Registration or by contacting the Data Holder directly.\n \nFor client applications using authorization code grants, end user authentication is performed using a username and authentication credentials provided by the Data Holder. A healthcare organization may reuse existing patient portal credentials for this purpose, in which case the authenticated username maps to a unique patient portal user on the resource holder’s side. The end user should obtain these credentials directly from the Data Holder healthcare organizations from which they wish to access data.\n \n Note: Usernames and authentication credentials are established and reset by the Data Holder.\n \n When an end user is directed to the authorization endpoint, the user will be presented with a login screen where they can enter their credentials for the healthcare organization they are accessing. During the authorization process, the user may also reduce the scopes that will be granted to the application. If the correct credentials are supplied and the end user grants access to the client application, an authorization code will be returned to the client that the client application can use to obtain an access token through the token endpoint as described in RFC 6749. For client credentials flow, the application obtains an access token directly from the token endpoint without user interaction.\n \nEach healthcare organization has a unique base URL that is used to access its Authorization Server. The required endpoint URLs are of the following form: \n \n \n Endpoint URL \n \n Authorization \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/authz \n \n Token \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/token \n \n Registration \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/register \n \n Manage \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/manage \n \n Revoke \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/revoke \n \n \n \n The complete OAuth 2.0 endpoint URLs for a given FHIR endpoint are published in that Data Holder's well known SMART configuration endpoint, as per Section 4 of the HL7 SMART App Launch specification .\n \n Refresh tokens: Client apps that use the authorization code flow and can securely store refresh tokens may request a refresh token by including the offline_access scope in the initial authorization endpoint request. Note that the end user may remove this scope during the authorization process. If a refresh token is permitted, it will be returned together with the access token as per RFC 6749 , and can be exchanged for a new access token as per RFC 6749 . Refresh token lifetime defaults to 90 days (but may be longer or shorter depending on the Data Holder's institutional policies). Each refresh token may be used once; a replacement refresh token will be issued when the client app uses a valid refresh token to obtain a new access token. Note that refresh tokens are not issued to Client apps that support client credentials code flow as these clients can authenticate directly to the token endpoint to obtain a new access token.\n \n PKCE support: Proof Key for Code Exchange (PKCE) as defined by RFC 7636 is required for Client apps that use the authorization code flow. Client apps must use only the S256 code challenge method. Please refer to RFC 7636 for details on how to use PKCE in requests to the authorization and token endpoints.\n \nAll requests to the API must include the access token transmitted in the Authorization header of the HTTP request as a bearer token as illustrated in RFC 6750 . If the access token is missing, expired, or otherwise not valid for the requested operation, the API will return a 401 Unauthorized or 403 Forbidden response.\n \n 3. API Details \n a. Query a Specific Data Category Resource \n Client software must be capable of making HTTPS RESTful requests in accordance with the FHIR specification and consuming the FHIR Resources required by the United States Core Data for Interoperability ( USCDI V3 ) or the FHIR Bulk Data Access specification. \n \n Resources returned conform to the resource profiles for HL7 FHIR R4 defined in the HL7 FHIR US Core Implementation Guide Release 6.1.0 found at https://hl7.org/fhir/us/core/STU6.1 . All of the US Core Resource Profiles are supported except the optional US Core Medication Profile, as this API uses inline medication codes to represent medications instead of optional References to separate Medication resources.\n \n General specifications for FHIR resources and the associated data elements can be found at https://hl7.org/fhir/R4/resourcelist.html for other resource types without a US Core profile.\n \n Additional information for each required FHIR Resource or Data Element used to represent each data category can be found at the page above. For example, the Patient.name element is found at:\n https://hl7.org/fhir/R4/patient-definitions.html#Patient.name . The US Core FHIR IG profiles found at https://hl7.org/fhir/us/core/STU6.1/profiles-and-extensions.html provide additional information on the meanings of elements and codes used by these profiles.\n \n Choices of data types: The US Core resource profiles permit a number of data types for certain resource elements as indicated by the [x] notation in the corresponding element names. Introduction \nThis guide is written for third party developers, including patients, who are developing software applications for accessing Protected Health Information (PHI) based on this public API documentation which allows applications to query health data secured by a Data Holder. Data Holders wishing to publish such a public-facing API should have their Health IT vendor register as a developer integrator of EMR Direct Interoperability Engine services at https://www.emrdirect.com/subscribe-developer . \nALWAYS KEEP IN MIND THAT ONLINE DATA TRANSFER IS NOT A SUBSTITUTE FOR PERSON-TO-PERSON COMMUNICATION OF URGENT OR CRITICAL MEDICAL INFORMATION. \nThis documentation contains general information and important security information. General information will be marked as:\n Note: \n and important security information will be marked as: \n Important: \n \n 2. General Concepts \n a. Application Access Requests \n The API is a read-only RESTful HL7 FHIR® R4 API and follows the syntax described at https://hl7.org/fhir/R4/http.html . \nAll RESTful data access requests will be in the following format, in which the [base] URL will need to be obtained from the Data Holder, either directly or via directory information:\n GET [base]/[resource-specific parameters]… \n FHIR Bulk Data Access Group export is also supported as described in the Bulk Data Access IG. \n b.\tConnecting to the server \nThe server is accessed by clients through an https connection.\n Important: Local customer security policies must be in place to prevent unauthorized monitoring or eavesdropping of connections to the server. \n Important: Only TLS 1.2 connections are accepted. All plaintext connections will be refused. \n Note: Please limit your connection frequency to a value appropriate for your use case. Connection attempts which are more frequent than permitted by the bandwidth allocation for the data resource are not allowed. \n c. Authentication – Obtaining an Access Token \nPrior to making API requests, the client application must obtain an Access Token from the associated Authorization Server. The client software must support either the OAuth 2.0 authorization code grant or client credentials grant flow as detailed in RFC 6749 . For authorization code flow, a client ID and client secret or a client ID and private key JWT can be used for authentication. If the client application does not have a client ID, the client application may obtain a client ID and (when required), client secret, using the dynamic client registration protocol by submitting the required client information to the registration endpoint as detailed in RFC 7591 , by accessing the registration endpoint URL with a browser and completing the online form, by completing the manual registration process at the EMR Direct website, or by using UDAP Dynamic Client Registration . Please see section 3(j) below for the list Client app attributes required for registration. For client credentials flow, a client ID and registered public key are required. Public keys for client credentials grant access may be registered using UDAP Dynamic Client Registration or by contacting the Data Holder directly.\n \nFor client applications using authorization code grants, end user authentication is performed using a username and authentication credentials provided by the Data Holder. A healthcare organization may reuse existing patient portal credentials for this purpose, in which case the authenticated username maps to a unique patient portal user on the resource holder’s side. The end user should obtain these credentials directly from the Data Holder healthcare organizations from which they wish to access data.\n \n Note: Usernames and authentication credentials are established and reset by the Data Holder.\n \n When an end user is directed to the authorization endpoint, the user will be presented with a login screen where they can enter their credentials for the healthcare organization they are accessing. During the authorization process, the user may also reduce the scopes that will be granted to the application. If the correct credentials are supplied and the end user grants access to the client application, an authorization code will be returned to the client that the client application can use to obtain an access token through the token endpoint as described in RFC 6749. For client credentials flow, the application obtains an access token directly from the token endpoint without user interaction.\n \nEach healthcare organization has a unique base URL that is used to access its Authorization Server. The required endpoint URLs are of the following form: \n \n \n Endpoint URL \n \n Authorization \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/authz \n \n Token \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/token \n \n Registration \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/register \n \n Manage \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/manage \n \n Revoke \t https://[baseOAuthURL]/revoke \n \n \n \n The complete OAuth 2.0 endpoint URLs for a given FHIR endpoint are published in that Data Holder's well known SMART configuration endpoint, as per Section 4 of the HL7 SMART App Launch specification .\n \n Refresh tokens: Client apps that use the authorization code flow and can securely store refresh tokens may request a refresh token by including the offline_access scope in the initial authorization endpoint request. Note that the end user may remove this scope during the authorization process. If a refresh token is permitted, it will be returned together with the access token as per RFC 6749 , and can be exchanged for a new access token as per RFC 6749 . Refresh token lifetime defaults to 90 days (but may be longer or shorter depending on the Data Holder's institutional policies). Each refresh token may be used once; a replacement refresh token will be issued when the client app uses a valid refresh token to obtain a new access token. Note that refresh tokens are not issued to Client apps that support client credentials code flow as these clients can authenticate directly to the token endpoint to obtain a new access token.\n \n PKCE support: Proof Key for Code Exchange (PKCE) as defined by RFC 7636 is required for Client apps that use the authorization code flow. The page exclusively covers the FHIR API (g(7)/g(9) criteria), not the b(10) EHI export.\n\n### Step 3: Follow the EMR Direct link\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.interopengine.com/2017/open-api-documentation\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o interopengine-2017-open-api-documentation.html\n```\n23,298 bytes. This is EMR Direct's Interoperability Engine 2017 documentation — a generic FHIR STU3 Ballot API guide for the Common Clinical Data Set (CCDS). It covers 20 CCDS data elements mapped to FHIR resources (Patient, Condition, MedicationStatement, AllergyIntolerance, Observation, Procedure, CareTeam, Immunization, Device, CarePlan, Goal). Authentication is OAuth 2.0 with patient portal credentials. Last updated February 12, 2020.\n\nThis is **not EHI export documentation.** It is g(7)/g(9) Application Access documentation.\n\n### Step 4: Check for newer interopengine versions\nThe interopengine.com homepage links to three API doc versions: 2017, 2021, and 2026. Downloaded the 2026 version:\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.interopengine.com/2026/open-api-documentation\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o interopengine-2026-open-api-documentation.html\n```\n41,219 bytes. This is significantly more detailed: FHIR R4, US Core IG Release 6.1.0, USCDI V3 support, SMART App Launch, FHIR Bulk Data Access Group export, PKCE, UDAP Dynamic Client Registration. It covers ~29 resource types.\n\nThis is g(10) Standardized API documentation. It mentions Bulk Data Access but **only for the US Core resource types** — no mention of b(10) EHI export or non-USCDI data.\n\n### Step 5: Check the ONC Certified HIT / Cures Update page\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://mednetmedical.com/2015-Cures-Update.html\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o mednet-2015-cures-update.html\n```\n48,159 bytes. Lists all 43 certified criteria. Under criterion (b)(10), it simply states:\n> (b)(10): **Electronic Health Information (EHI) export**\n\nNo link to EHI export documentation, no description of the export format, no data dictionary. It's just a certification listing page.\n\n### Step 6: Download the Price Transparency PDF\n```bash\ncurl -sL \"https://www.mednetmedical.com/price-transparency/Price_Transparency_emr4MD_v9.10.pdf\" -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' -o Price_Transparency_emr4MD_v9.10.pdf\n```\n956,711 bytes, 5 pages. Under 170.315(b)(10), it states:\n> **Electronic Health Information (EHI) export**: \"This functionality allows a practice to create individual and group exports of PHI without programming intervention.\"\n> **Cost**: \"Included in base service agreement, subscription fee plus Subscription fee from 3rd party - EMR Direct, one time implementation fee and annual subscription charged.\"\n\nThis is the only description of the b(10) EHI export found anywhere. It confirms:\n1. The export exists as a feature\n2. It requires EMR Direct (third-party dependency)\n3. It supports individual and group exports\n4. It does not require programming intervention\n\nBut there is **no documentation of the export format, no data dictionary, no field definitions, no schema**, and no instructions for how to use the export.\n\n### Step 7: Search for additional EHI documentation\nSearched the vendor's site for any pages containing \"ehi\", \"export\", \"b(10)\", \"data dictionary\", or \"bulk\":\n- Checked common paths: /ehi, /ehi-export, /EHI_Export, /b10, /bulk-export, /data-dictionary — all returned 404\n- Searched the interopengine.com site for EHI-specific pages — none found\n- The homepage links to \"AppStudio\" at appstudio.interopengine.com but this is a developer portal, not EHI export documentation\n\nNo additional EHI export documentation was found anywhere on the vendor's site or the third-party partner's site.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nThe registered URL points to a minimal Open API page that redirects to EMR Direct's generic FHIR API documentation. **No EHI export (b)(10) documentation exists at the registered URL or anywhere else on the vendor's site.**\n\nWhat is documented:\n- **FHIR API (g(7)/g(9)/g(10))**: EMR Direct's Interoperability Engine provides a FHIR API covering the Common Clinical Data Set (2017 version, STU3) and US Core/USCDI V3 (2026 version, R4). This is standard (g)(10) API documentation — not EHI export.\n- **EHI Export feature existence**: The Price Transparency PDF confirms that a b(10) EHI export feature exists, allows individual and group exports of PHI without programming, and requires an EMR Direct subscription. But no format, schema, or field-level documentation is provided.\n\nWhat is not documented:\n- Export format (FHIR? C-CDA? CSV? Database dump? Unknown)\n- Data dictionary or field definitions\n- Which data domains are included in the export\n- How to initiate or receive the export\n- Sample data or examples\n- Schema files of any kind\n- Instructions for interpreting the exported data\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\n**Impossible to assess.** No data dictionary, schema, or format documentation exists for the b(10) export. The only statement is that the feature \"allows a practice to create individual and group exports of PHI without programming intervention.\"\n\nBased on the product research, emr4MD (as part of the 4MD360 suite) stores:\n- **Clinical**: Problems, medications, allergies, immunizations, vital signs, lab results, procedures, clinical notes, family history, social history, implantable devices, care plans, clinical decision support alerts\n- **Practice management/billing**: Scheduling, charges, claims, payments, copays, ERA, EOB, patient statements, ICD-10 billing\n- **E-prescribing**: Prescription history, refill requests (via DrFirst)\n- **Patient portal**: Secure messages, appointment requests, refill requests, patient-submitted health info\n- **Quality measures**: eCQM data for ~20 clinical quality measures\n\nThe FHIR API documentation (which is for g(10), not b(10)) only covers USCDI/US Core clinical data — approximately 20 standard data categories. If the b(10) export is simply a repackaging of the g(10) FHIR API output, it would miss:\n- All billing and financial data (charges, claims, payments, EOBs, patient statements)\n- Scheduling and appointment data\n- E-prescribing data beyond what's in MedicationRequest\n- Patient portal messages and requests\n- Custom clinical templates and disease management data\n- Document scans and imports\n- Quality measure submissions\n\nHowever, since there is no documentation at all, we cannot determine whether the export actually covers these domains or not.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\n**Unknown.** The Price Transparency PDF mentions EMR Direct is required, suggesting the export may go through the Interoperability Engine — potentially using FHIR. But the registered URL points to the g(7)/g(9) FHIR STU3 API documentation, which is a patient-level API, not a bulk export mechanism.\n\nThe 2026 interopengine docs mention FHIR Bulk Data Access Group export, but this is for g(10) and only covers US Core resource types.\n\nWithout documentation, a third party could not reconstruct a patient record from this export because the format is not specified.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Virtually nonexistent for b(10).** The registered URL does not contain any b(10) EHI export documentation. It points to generic g(7)/g(9) FHIR API docs provided by a third-party partner (EMR Direct). The only mention of b(10) is a single sentence in the Price Transparency PDF.\n\nThe FHIR API documentation itself (for g(10)) is of reasonable quality — it specifies resource types, search parameters, authentication, data type choices, and US Core IG conformance. But this is irrelevant to the b(10) EHI export requirement.\n\nA developer could not implement an import of EHI export data based on the available documentation because the export format is entirely undocumented.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Data dictionary**: None\n- **Field-level definitions**: None\n- **Data types/value sets**: None (except for the FHIR API which is a different system)\n- **Relationships between entities**: None\n- **Worked examples**: None\n- **Schema files**: None\n- **Export instructions**: None\n- **Versioning/change history**: None\n\nThis is a textbook example of a vendor treating b(10) as a compliance checkbox rather than a substantive documentation requirement. The vendor is certified for b(10), but the registered documentation URL points to their g(7)/g(9) FHIR API documentation — a completely different certification criterion.\n\n## Access Summary\n- Final URL (after redirects): https://mednetmedical.com/Open_API.html (no redirect)\n- Status: found (but content is g(7)/g(9) API docs, not b(10) EHI export docs)\n- Required browser: no\n- Navigation complexity: direct_link (single page with one external link)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\n- The registered URL contains no b(10) EHI export documentation whatsoever\n- The URL links to EMR Direct's Interoperability Engine API docs, which cover g(7)/g(9)/g(10) FHIR API, not b(10) EHI export\n- No alternative EHI documentation paths were found on the vendor's site\n- The vendor appears to conflate their g(7)/g(9) FHIR API documentation with their b(10) EHI export documentation requirement\n- The only evidence that a b(10) export feature exists is a one-sentence description in the Price Transparency PDF\n- EMR Direct (the third-party partner) does not appear to publish b(10)-specific documentation either\n- The MedNet page still references the 2017 interopengine docs (FHIR STU3), even though a 2026 version (FHIR R4, US Core 6.1) exists, suggesting the page is not actively maintained\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":730,"cache_read_input_tokens":95305,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":730},"output_tokens":4,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"edb7e917-16c3-4c90-9004-85bee5b1710b","uuid":"497cb26f-ae84-4962-b071-c75a1e5fa613"}
